Hi, thank you so much for fitting me in. I really appreciate it. Um... I'm here because... well, I'm hoping it's just stress. I'm a chief executive, so there's always a lot on my plate, and I don't want to sound like I'm not handling my responsibilities well. 

But... I've been having this issue where I keep hearing people talking about me. Usually, it's the board members or my staff, and they're saying I'm ruining the company. I know how crazy that sounds to say out loud, I really do. But I'll hear them right through the conference room walls, or even coming from my cell phone when it's completely turned off. It just... it sounds so incredibly clear that I actually catch myself answering them back, and then I look around and realize no one else in the room heard a thing.

Do you think... could that just be a severe nervous tic from all the pressure I'm under? I really hope it doesn't make me seem totally unprofessional to you... I'm usually very put-together.

I really hope it doesn't make me seem totally unprofessional to you... I'm usually very put-together.\n\nDoctor: Did these voices and unusual behaviors start suddenly over just a few days or weeks, rather than gradually building up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it wasn't like a switch flipped overnight or anything. Just feeling a bit more stressed, maybe thinking I heard something when I was working late at the office by myself... and then it just sort of built up gradually until it became what it is now. \n\nI really tried to manage it as it was happening, I didn't want to let it get out of hand. I guess I'd have to say things have slipped a little bit. I really try my best, I promise I do, but... it's been hard to keep up. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm failing at my job, but... I haven't been as reliable with running my division lately. Making those basic executive decisions has just felt so overwhelming with the voices, and... well, my senior staff have been so kind, they've quietly taken over a lot of the work I usually handle myself. I feel terrible putting that on them. \n\nAnd at home... I've been leaving things unfinished, or just not starting them at all. My husband has had to step up and manage almost all the household responsibilities. I feel so guilty about it. \n\nI'm usually very capable, I really am. Do you think... does that sound like something that can happen when you're just severely burnt out? I'm hoping I can get back on track soon so I don't let anyone else down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the voices or the feeling that people are talking about you happened only when your mood was also clearly unusually high or unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think they're connected like that. I just... I didn't want to make a fuss or be a burden to anyone. Is three months... is that a very bad sign? I hope I'm not a hopeless case.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when your behavior became very disorganized or strange, in a way other people noticed?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well... that's a little hard to admit. But... yes, I suppose people have noticed. \n\nSometimes, if I'm in a big executive meeting, or... or hosting one of our gallery events, I'll just... I'll suddenly stand up. I know how crazy that sounds to say out loud, I really do. But I'll hear them right through the conference room walls, or even coming from my cell phone when it's completely turned off. It just... it sounds so incredibly clear that I actually catch myself answering them back, and then I look around and realize no one else in the room heard a thing. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit, honestly. Is... is that something you see often with high-stress jobs? I'm hoping I'm just overworked.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start hearing these voices?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess if I'm being completely honest, the ones coming from the walls and my phone... it's been happening for about the past three months. I'm sorry I didn't come in sooner, I just... I really didn't want to make a big deal out of it, and I kept hoping it would just go away if I managed my schedule better. I hope I'm explaining this okay. Does three months sound like it could just be a temporary burnout phase?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period of at least a week when you felt unusually energized or euphoric and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I definitely haven't felt euphoric, or anything like that. Honestly, I'm usually just quite tired by the end of the day from trying to keep up with all the demands at work. \n\nI always try to make sure I get a proper night's sleep so I can be sharp and presentable for the company... I try to be very responsible about my routine, so I haven't had any times where I just didn't need to sleep. Is... is it a good sign that I haven't experienced that? I really hope I'm giving you the right information, I just want to be as helpful as possible.\n\nDoctor: Have your work or day-to-day functioning been getting noticeably worse during this time?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I'd have to say things have slipped a little bit. I really try my best, I promise I do, but... it's been hard to keep up. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm failing at my job, but... I haven't been as reliable with running my division lately. Making those basic executive decisions has just felt so overwhelming with the voices, and... well, my senior staff have been so kind, they've quietly taken over a lot of the work I usually handle myself. I feel terrible putting that on them. \n\nAnd at home... I've been leaving things unfinished, or just not starting them at all. My husband has had to step up and manage almost all the household responsibilities. I feel so guilty about it. \n\nI'm usually very capable, I really am. Do you think... does that sound like something that can happen when you're just severely burnt out? I'm hoping I can get back on track soon so I don't let anyone else down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the voices or the feeling that people are talking about you happened only when your mood was also clearly unusually high or unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think they're connected like that. I mean, I've definitely been feeling a little sad lately, mostly just because I feel so guilty about letting my team and my husband down... but I wouldn't say I've been unusually depressed or anything. Just normal stress, I promise. \n\nAnd the... the voices, they happen even on days when I wake up feeling completely fine and ready to tackle my schedule. They don't seem to care what kind of mood I'm in at all. Even when I'm trying my absolute hardest to be positive and put on a good face for everyone, they're still there. \n\nI hope I'm answering that clearly enough. I really want to give you the right information. Does... does that help rule out anything serious?\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or beliefs about people talking about you been present continuously for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, they have. I really wish I could say it was just a brief thing, but... it's been going on for a while now. \n\nI'd say... maybe about three months? That the voices have been coming from the walls, or... or through my phone. I really tried to ignore them at first, hoping they would just go away on their own if I worked harder and focused on my job, but they've been pretty consistent. \n\nI'm sorry, I know three months probably sounds like a long time to let this go on without getting help. I just... I didn't want to make a fuss or be a burden to anyone. Is three months... is that a very bad sign? I hope I'm not a hopeless case.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when your behavior became very disorganized or strange, in a way other people noticed?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well... that's a little hard to admit. I... I guess I wouldn't call it *very* strange, maybe just... a bit quirky? Or like a nervous habit from being so overwhelmed? But... yes, I suppose people have noticed. \n\nSometimes, if I'm in a big executive meeting, or... or hosting one of our gallery events, I'll just... I'll suddenly stand up. And I'll start rearranging the pens on the conference table, or moving the name cards around into these... these specific patterns. Or if someone is speaking, I might just repeat the exact phrase they just said right out loud. \n\nAnd sometimes I'll catch myself just... holding my hands up in the air, almost like I'm presenting something really important to the room, but... but I'm not actually giving a presentation. \n\nI always realize afterward that it made absolutely no sense for the room, and I feel so incredibly embarrassed. I really am trying my hardest to stop doing it, I promise. Do you think... could that just be a severe nervous tic from all the pressure I'm under? I really hope it doesn't make me seem totally unprofessional to you... I'm usually very put-together.\n\nDoctor: Did these voices and unusual behaviors start suddenly over just a few days or weeks, rather than gradually building up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it wasn't like a switch flipped overnight or anything. I don't think it was that sudden. \n\nIt felt more like... like it just slowly crept up on me over a while. At first, it was just little things, you know? Just feeling a bit more stressed, maybe thinking I heard something when I was working late at the office by myself... and then it just sort of built up gradually until it became what it is now. \n\nI really tried to manage it as it was happening, I didn't want to let it get out of hand. I hope that's the right answer? I mean, I hope it's not a bad thing that I didn't notice it all at once... I promise I was trying to pay attention to my health. Does a gradual start make it easier to treat?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast so other people can hear them?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, I don't think I've ever felt anything quite like that. I mean, my thoughts are just... my own, you know? \n\nSometimes I might get a little distracted or, or lose my train of thought during a meeting, but I think that's just normal executive stress, right? I certainly don't feel like anyone is putting thoughts in there or taking them out. And I definitely don't think anyone can hear what I'm thinking... that would be terribly embarrassing, wouldn't it? \n\nI really try to keep my private thoughts to myself and just focus on being professional. So, no, nothing like that. I hope that's a good sign? That means I'm still doing okay in that department, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, recreational drugs, or new medications around the time these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: Oh! No, no, definitely not. I mean... well, I might have half a glass of wine at a charity gala or a networking event, just to be polite, you know? But I really don't drink much at all. \n\nAnd recreational drugs? Oh, never. I have a whole company depending on me, I would never do anything irresponsible like that. I really try to take my health seriously, I promise. And I haven't started any new medications either... just my usual daily vitamins. \n\nI've been very careful about what I put in my body. That's... that's a good thing, right? I hope it helps show that I'm really trying to do everything right and be responsible... I wouldn't want you to think I brought this on myself by taking something I shouldn't have.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods during these three months when the voices or unusual behavior completely went away?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not completely. I mean, I really wish I could say they did... but they've pretty much been there the whole time. \n\nSometimes they're quieter, you know? Like if I'm really focused on a big presentation or deep into some spreadsheets, I can almost ignore them and just power through. I really try my best not to let them interfere with my work. But... they never fully stop. \n\nI'm sorry, I know it would probably be better if I had some clear days to report. I really have been trying to push past it all and stay productive. Is it... is it really bad that they haven't stopped at all? I hope you don't think I'm not trying hard enough to get better.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the voices or suspicious beliefs were still present even though your mood was completely normal and there were no major depressive or manic symptoms?\n\nPatient: Um... let me think. Two weeks of just... just the voices, but feeling completely normal otherwise? \n\nWell, I don't really get depressed or... or manic, I don't think. I try to keep a very even keel, especially at work. You can't be flying off the handle or breaking down when you're running a company, right? So my mood is usually quite stable. I make sure of it.\n\nBut... the voices and the feeling that people are watching me... that's just kind of always there now, like I said. So I guess... yes? There have been weeks where my mood is perfectly fine, I'm getting my work done, I'm being pleasant with everyone... but the voices are still talking. \n\nI really do try not to let it affect my mood, though. I put on a smile and keep things professional. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing things as well as I can, given the circumstances.\n\nDoctor: During the past three months, have you had any period of at least two weeks when you felt markedly depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. Definitely not depressed. I mean... I really try to stay as positive as I can. You kind of have to, right? When you have a whole team looking up to you, you can't just... sit around feeling sad or sorry for yourself all day. \n\nI make sure to keep a smile on and keep moving forward, even with... well, with everything else going on. So, no, definitely no weeks of feeling down like that. I really try to be strong and keep a good attitude. That's... that's a good sign, isn't it? That I'm keeping my spirits up and not letting it get to me?\n\nDoctor: Have these voices and the other unusual experiences been going on for six months or more in total?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. Actually, it's... it's been quite a bit longer than six months. I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, because I know I probably should have come in sooner, but... it's been building for about two years and four months now. \n\nIt started right around the time of our big company expansion, when they put me in charge of the new arts-and-commerce division. I really just thought it was the stress of the new role at first, you know? I wanted to do a good job and prove I could handle the extra responsibility, so I just tried to push through it and not make a fuss. \n\nI hope you don't think I was being irresponsible by waiting so long to talk to someone about it. I really was just trying to keep everything running smoothly for my team and not let anyone down. It's understandable that I'd want to just focus on the work, isn't it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
